Implementation details:
Represent quadratic q(z)=a_q z² + b_q z + c.
q1: a=1,b=-2a_i,c=old1.
q2: a=-2,b=2(a_i-a_j),c=old2. (concave)
q3: a=1,b=2a_j,c=old3.
Candidates z set: lower=-a_j, upper=a_i.
For each quadratic with a_q >0 (q1,q3), vertex = -b_q/(2a_q) within [lower,upper]. q2 concave no min (max at endpoints but in max envelope could be important at crosses).

Local exact optimization via FFT line search for direction d:
Given current a, choose a direction d (sum 0, can be negative) such that a+td stays nonnegative. We can find best t by sampling/FFT and update. The SOTA LP gives direction. We can generate directions from gradient, random, template, and do line search. This might be powerful: full FFT per t.

Line search: For direction d (sum zero), t ∈ [-t_min, t_max] to keep nonnegative. Compute score at t via FFT. Use golden section? Objective may nonconvex but one-dimensional often unimodal if direction good. We can evaluate e.g., 20 points and take best. Number of directions limited.

Let's define:
```
def conv_square(f):
    size=1 << (2*len(f)-1).bit_length()
    F=np.fft.rfft(f,size)
    return np.fft.irfft(F*F,size)[:2*n-1]
```
For correlation r*f_valid:
```
def corr_valid(r, f): # r length 2n-1, f n, return length n: sum f[j] r[i+j]
    size=1 << (3*n-2).bit_length()
    R=np.fft.rfft(r,size)
    F=np.fft.rfft(f,size)
    c=np.fft.irfft(R*np.conj(F), size)
    return c[:n]
```
Check sign/shift. Test with r_k=a_{?}? For simple f=[a0,a1], r=[r0,r1,r2]. Desired h0=f0*r0+f1*r1; h1=f0*r1+f1*r2. Full correlate(r,f): output m=0: ∑ r[n]f[n] over n=0..1 =r0f0+r1f1. m=1: r1f0+r2f1. Good. So first n. Perfect.

Now, gradient for raw softmax:
- b=conv_square(f)
- weights w_k = softmax(b/T)
- grad = 2 * corr_valid(w, f)  (since w length 2n-1)
For log softmax:
- compute L=log(b+eps)
- w=softmax(L/T)
- r = w / b (clip)
- grad = 2 * corr_valid(r, f)

Mirror step on simplex:
```
def mirror_step(f, grad, step, base=None):
    # grad finite
    span=grad.max()-grad.min()
    if span small return f
    eta=step/span
    logf=np.log(f+eps) - eta*grad
    logf-=logf.max()
    fnew=np.exp(logf)
    fnew/=fnew.sum()
    return fnew
```
But if grad large for zero f, log(0) problem; add eps. We can maintain small floor floor=1e-15? We can set f = np.maximum(f, 0) but many zeros. Maybe keep all positive by adding small floor and normalizing: f = (f*(1-ε)+ε)*? Or maintain f_min=1e-12. Since target nonnegative, zeros allowed, but interior methods prefer positive. We can add tiny floor at end and normalize (score negligible). e.g., f = np.maximum(f, 1e-12); f/=f.sum(). For n=6000, adding 6e-9 sum negligible. This avoids log zero.

Another approach: Use subgradient of exact max: Define active weights w_active over lags with b > M - tol. Flat. grad=2corr(w_active normalized, f). This directly targets current max. Exact active gradient: For active lags, p_k=1/|A|; grad_i=2∑_{k∈A} f_{k-i}/|A|. This is the subgradient of max. Multiplicative update with this gradient decreases active coefficients in first order. Let's implement a generic optimizer with stages:
- Normalize f sum1.
- Compute b, peak.
- For iterations until time:
   - progress p = elapsed/time_budget
   - Temperature for top weights: choose threshold = peak * (1 - temp_factor) maybe. We can select weights = exp((b - peak)/T_rel) with T_rel from peak*0.1 down to 1e-6. This is raw softmax centered at peak (not all lags), focusing near max. Previous centered at max. Good.
   - grad=2corr(weights,f).
   - Use mirror step with step size maybe 0.01-0.2.
   - Evaluate score every 20-100 iterations; keep best.
   - If no improvement for some evals, perturb: f = (1-ε)f + ε*random_seed (e.g., inverse sqrt), or apply random multiplicative noise.

We can test multiple seeds. But to beat 1.5, maybe need more global.

Let's design final code:
```
import numpy as np
import math
import time

def _fft_score(f, max_n=None):
    f=np.asarray(f,float)
    n=len(f)
    if n==0: return np.inf
    s=float(np.sum(f))
    if s<1e-12: return np.inf
    # To avoid huge, if n large? use FFT
    size=1
    while size < 2*n-1: size <<=1
    F=np.fft.rfft(f,size)
    b=np.fft.irfft(F*F,size)[:2*n-1]
    m=float(b.max())
    return 2.0*n*m/(s*s)
```
This matches evaluate for nonneg values within [0,1000]. For n=12288 size=32768 FFT ~fast. We can evaluate many.

```
def _log_soft_grad(f, T):
    n=len(f)
    b=conv_square(f)
    L=np.log(np.maximum(b,1e-300))
    Tsafe=max(T,1e-12)
    cs=L/Tsafe
    cs-=cs.max()
    p=np.exp(cs); p/=p.sum()
    r=p/np.maximum(b,1e-300)
    h=corr_valid(r,f)
    return 2*h, b
```

Let's design `_polish_cosine(f, time_budget)`:
```
def _polish_sequence(f, time_budget, seed=123):
    f=f.copy()
    # floor
    floor=1e-10/max(1,len(f))
    f=np.maximum(f, floor)
    f=f/f.sum()
    best_score=_score_fast(f)
    best=f.copy()
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    start=time.monotonic()
    T=0.3
    step=0.05
    no_improve=0
    it=0
    while time.monotonic()<start+time_budget:
       b=_fft_conv_square(f)
       # log soft weights
       L=np.log(np.maximum(b,1e-300))
       cs=L/T; cs-=cs.max(); p=np.exp(cs); s=p.sum(); if s<=0: p=zeros; p[argmax]=1; else p/=s
       r=p/np.maximum(b,1e-300)
       h=_corr_valid(r,f)
       grad=2*h
       f=_mirror_step(f,grad,step)
       it+=1
       # anneal/step schedule
       if it % 100 ==0:
           sc=_score_fast(f)
           if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
              best=f.copy(); best_score=sc; no_improve=0
           else:
              no_improve+=1
              if no_improve>5:
                 # perturbation
                 f=(1-0.01)*f +0.01*rng.dirichlet(np.ones(len(f))*0.01)
                 f=f/f.sum()
                 no_improve=0
       if it % 1000 ==0:
           # reset T based on score? maybe
           step *=0.99
    # final evaluate
    sc=_score_fast(f)
    if sc<best_score: ...
    # Remove floor? We can clip negatives and not normalize? Keep normalized.
    best=np.maximum(best,0.0)
    return best, best_score
```
Converging maybe. But log gradient may increase peaks if step too large. The polish may oscillate. But since we only output if internal score improves over pre, safe. Time budget small (e.g., 60s) if not needed. Could skip if best<1.40 to save time. Maybe include but not essential.

Need implement `_corr_valid` efficiently:
```
def _corr_valid(r, f):
    n=len(f)
    size=1 << (3*n-2).bit_length()
    R=np.fft.rfft(r,size)
    F=np.fft.rfft(f,size)
    c=np.fft.irfft(R*np.conj(F), size)
    return c[:n]
```
For n=12288, size=65536? 3n-2=36862, next power 65536. FFT okay. If many iterations, each eval includes 2 FFT size 65536 plus conv size 32768. Might be heavy. Polish 60s maybe few hundred iterations. Fine.

```
def _next_pow2(m):
    if m <=1: return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()
```

```
def _conv_square(f):
    n=len(f)
    size=_next_pow2(2*n-1)
    F=np.fft.rfft(f,size)
    b=np.fft.irfft(F*F,size)
    return b[:2*n-1]
```

Power sequence:
```
def _power_seq(n, p=0.5, alpha=1.0):
    i=np.arange(1,n+1,dtype=float)
    f=i**(-p)
    # optional endpoint taper: f *= (1 - 0.5*np.cos? )
    f=np.maximum(f,0)
    s=f.sum()
    return f/s
```

Implement `_mirror_step(f, grad, step)`:
```
def _mirror_step(f, grad, step):
    n=len(f)
    span=grad.max()-grad.min()
    if span <1e-18: return f
    eta=step/span
    logf=np.log(np.maximum(f,1e-300)) - eta*grad
    logf-=logf.max()
    new=np.exp(logf)
    s=new.sum()
    if s <=1e-300: return f
    new /= s
    return new
```
`grad` could have large values; span normalize. Step choose 0.1 maybe.

`_polish_sequence`:
```
def _polish_sequence(f0, budget_s, seed=123):
   if budget_s <=0: return f0.copy(), _score_fast(f0)
   f=f0.astype(float).copy()
   f=np.maximum(f,0.0)
   s=f.sum()
   if s<1e-12: f=np.ones(len(f)); s=1
   f=f/s
   # floor for optimization
   fl=1e-10/len(f)
   f=np.maximum(f, fl); f=f/f.sum()
   best=f.copy(); best_score=_score_fast(f)
   rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
   start=time.monotonic()
   T=0.2
   step=0.05
   it=0
   noe=0
   while time.monotonic() - start < budget_s:
       b=_conv_square(f)
       pos=b>1e-300
       if not pos.any(): break
       L=np.full_like(b, -1e30)
       L[pos]=np.log(b[pos])
       cs=L/T
       # subtract max finite
       maxL=np.max(cs)
       cs=cs-maxL
       p=np.exp(cs)
       ps=p.sum()
       if ps<=0: p=np.zeros_like(b); p[int(np.argmax(b))]=1
       else: p/=ps
       r=np.zeros_like(b)
       r[pos]=p[pos]/b[pos]
       h=_corr_valid(r,f)
       grad=2.0*h
       f=_mirror_step(f, grad, step)
       # ensure nonnegative, normalize
       f=np.maximum(f,0.0); fs=f.sum(); if fs<...; f/=fs
       it+=1
       # schedule: reduce T slowly, reduce step
       if it % 200 ==0:
           sc=_score_fast(f)
           if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
               best=f.copy(); best_score=sc; noe=0
           else:
               noe+=1
               if noe>3:
                   # random blend with original or random
                   mix = min(0.03, 0.005*noe)
                   f = (1-mix)*f + mix*f0
                   f=np.maximum(f,fl); f/=f.sum()
                   noe=0
           # adjust T
           T = max(0.02, T*0.98)
           step = max(0.005, step*0.995)
       if it % 500 ==0 and T <=0.02: break
   sc=_score_fast(f)
   if sc<best_score: best=f.copy(); best_score=sc
   # remove floor? Keep normalized nonnegative
   best=np.maximum(best,0.0); bs=best.sum(); if bs>0: best/=bs
   return best,best_score
```

Detailed code:
```
import numpy as np
import math
import time

def _next_pow2(m):
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()

def _conv_square(f):
    n = len(f)
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=float)
    size = _next_pow2(2*n - 1)
    F = np.fft.rfft(f, size)
    b = np.fft.irfft(F * F, size)
    return b[:2*n - 1]

def _score_fast(f):
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=float)
    n = f.size
    if n == 0:
        return float('inf')
    s = float(np.sum(f))
    if s <= 1e-12 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return float('inf')
    b = _conv_square(f)
    if b.size == 0:
        return float('inf')
    m = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return float('inf')
    return float(2.0 * n * m / (s * s))
```
For corr:
```
def _corr_valid(r, f):
    n=len(f)
    size=_next_pow2(3*n-2)
    R=np.fft.rfft(r,size)
    F=np.fft.rfft(f,size)
    c=np.fft.irfft(R*np.conj(F), size)
    return c[:n]
```
Need note: If r length 2n-1, f length n, full corr length 3n-2. Good.

Normalize:
```
def _normalize_nonneg(f):
    f=np.asarray(f,dtype=float).copy()
    f=np.where(np.isfinite(f), f, 0.0)
    f=np.maximum(f,0.0)
    s=f.sum()
    if s <=1e-12:
        return None
    return f/s
```

Trig candidate:
```
def _trig_candidate(n, amp, beta=0.0):
    if n < 2:
        return None
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=float) / float(n - 1)
    f = 1.0 + amp * np.cos(math.pi * x)
    if beta != 0.0:
        f = f + beta * np.cos(2.0 * math.pi * x)
    f = np.maximum(f, 0.0)
    return _normalize_nonnorm(f)
```
Maybe all zero? no.

Power candidate:
```
def _power_candidate(n, p=0.5, alpha=1.0):
    i=np.arange(1,n+1,dtype=float)
    f=i**(-p)
    # multiply by (1 - 0.5*x?) Could keep.
    return _normalize_nonneg(f)
```

Algorithm outline:
```
def _active_mirror_polish(f, budget):
   f normalized sum1, floor small
   current_score=score_fast(f)
   best=f
   active_thresh=0.999
   top_k=10
   eta=0.05
   stall=0
   while time< budget:
       b=conv_square(f)
       M=b.max()
       # select active: b > M*0.999, up to top_k by value
       idx=np.argsort(b)[-top_k:]
       # weight maybe proportional to (b-M+tol)
       w = np.zeros_like(b)
       mask=b > M*(1-1e-4)
       if count<2: active=idx[0]
       w[active]=1/len
       h=corr_valid(w, f)
       grad=2*h
       grad = grad - np.mean(grad) # project to sum
       span=grad.max()-grad.min()
       if span small: perturb
       logf=log(f+eps) - eta*grad/span
       fnew=exp(logf); normalize
       sc=score_fast(fnew)
       if sc < current_score - 1e-8:
           f=fnew; current_score=sc; best...; stall=0
       else:
           # try smaller eta (eta/2, /4) maybe
           accepted=False
           for factor in [0.5,0.25,0.1]:
               ... if improve accept
           if not accepted:
               stall++; if stall>20: perturb random or change active threshold
       eta schedule
```

Let's write robust mirror polish:
```
def _mirror_refine(f0, budget_s, seed, initial_eta=0.1):
    f=f0 normalized, add floor 1e-12/n
    best_score=score_fast(f); best=f.copy()
    rng=...
    top_k=min(20, 2*n-1)
    eta=initial_eta
    progress_time=0; total=budget
    last_eval_best=...
    it=0
    stall=0
    while time<deadline:
       b=conv_square(f)
       M=b.max()
       # weights: top lags with flat/softmax
       # choose thresholds: top 10, plus b > M*(1-0.0001)
       idx=np.argpartition(b, -top_k)[-top_k:]
       w=np.zeros(len(b))
       vals=b[idx]
       # softmax with small temperature relative to M
       temp = M * (0.001 if it<... else 1e-5)
       p=np.exp((vals - vals.max())/ (max(temp,1e-30)))
       p/=p.sum()
       w[idx]=p
       h=_corr_valid(w, f)
       grad=2.0*h
       g=grad - np.mean(grad)
       gscale=np.max(np.abs(g))
       if gscale <1e-18: break/perturb
       g = g/gscale
       cand=f.copy()
       for e in [eta, eta*0.5, eta*0.25]:
           lf=np.log(cand) - e*g
           lf-=lf.max(); nf=exp(lf); nf/=nf.sum()
           sc=score_fast(nf)
           if sc < best_score - 1e-9:
               f=nf; best...; break
       else:
           # no improvement; smaller eta or perturb
           eta *=0.8? or random perturb
           stall++
           if stall>5: f=(1-0.001)f + 0.001 random; ...
       eta = max(0.005, eta*0.999?)
```
This only accepts if score improves; if many attempts fail, progress stalls. We can allow non-improving moves that reduce smooth surrogate? Maybe.


    - Try several eta values in a list, applying mirror step to current (or best) and evaluating score; accept best improvement. If none improve, perturb current with a random smooth direction (Fourier or polynomial) with small mix, and continue.
    - Periodically update best.

Mirror `_refine_mirror` details:
```
def _refine_mirror(f0, deadline, seed=1):
    n=len(f0); rng=...
    f=normalize with floor=1e-10/n
    best=f; best_s=score_fast(f)
    top_k=8 or 16
    eta=0.08
    stall=0
    last_print=0
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
        b=conv_square(f)
        M=b.max()
        # weights: choose top_lags = argpartition top_k
        idx=np.argpartition(b, -top_k)[-top_k:]
        vals=b[idx]
        # temperature: M * (0.01 + 0.01* (stall?)) maybe
        temp = max(M*0.02, 1e-300)
        p=np.exp((vals-vals.max())/temp); p/=p.sum()
        w=np.zeros(len(b)); w[idx]=p
        h=corr_valid(w,f)
        grad=2*h - 2*np.mean(h) # mean subtract (since derivative of logS? S fixed? just center)
        gscale=max(abs(grad).max(), eps)
        grad=grad/gscale
        # try eta list [eta, eta*0.5]
        improved=False
        for e in (eta, eta*0.5):
            nf=f.copy()
            lf=np.log(nf) - e*grad
            nf=exp(normalize)
            if nf finite:
               sc=score_fast(nf)
               if sc < best_s - 1e-12:
                   best=nf.copy(); best_s=sc; f=nf; improved=True; break
        if improved:
            stall=0
            eta = min(0.2, eta*1.05)
        else:
            stall += 1
            eta = max(0.003, eta*0.85)
            if stall > 5:
                # perturb around best
                alpha = min(0.05, 0.005*stall)
                dirn=random_smooth...
                f = normalize(max((1-alpha)*best + alpha*normalize(exp(dirn)),0))
                # maybe evaluate? not necessary
                stall=0
        # periodic print
    # final: also try small random restarts? no
    return best, best_s
```
Problem: If gradient only top 8, direction may reduce top peaks but may increase others; score check handles. Step sizes small.

Could use `h=corr_valid(w,f)` with w sum1; gradient span maybe small; normalizing by max may create steps e up to 0.2 log units. Good.

Maybe use log-sum-exp gradient over all lags with temperature relative to M. Instead of top_k, compute weights for all b: cs=(b-M)/temp; for b much smaller, weights negligible. This highlights active. But computing exp length 2n each iter okay. Use `w = exp((b - b.max())/temp)`; w sum. temp from M*0.05 to M*1e-4. If temp small, many underflow; fine. Then h=corr_valid(w/f? no raw). This uses all lags near max. Top_k is cheaper and sparse but corr_valid FFT dense anyway. We can use top_k to reduce large values.

The gradient of score not log? Since S fixed, okay. Maybe we should use gradient of log b weights divided by b? Let's include a mode option: raw vs log.
- raw weights w; h=corr_valid(w,f); grad=2h.
- log weights: r=w/ b; h=corr_valid(r,f); grad=2h. Which better? Use raw for max. We can try both in alternation or select based on progress. Simpler use raw.

Perturbation direction generation:
```
def _smooth_direction(n, rng, modes=8):
    y=np.zeros(n)
    x=np.arange(n)/n
    for m in range(1,modes+1):
       amp=rng.normal(0,1)/(m+1)
       phase=rng.uniform(0,2π)
       y += amp*np.cos(2π*m*x+phase)
    y -= y.mean()
    # optional taper
    y += rng.normal(0,1)*(np.linspace(0,1,n)-0.5)
    return y
```
Exp direction: `np.exp(0.1*direction)` maybe.

Subspace parameter optimizer:
```
def _subspace_refine(f0, deadline, seed, D=10, max_steps=20000):
    n=len(f0); f=f0 normalized; best=f; best_s=score_fast(f)
    base_log=np.log(f+eps)
    directions=[]
    for r in range(D): directions.append(_smooth_direction(n,rng))
    for dir: normalize so max abs=1 and mean0.
    theta=np.zeros(D)
    step=0.05
    improvable=True
    while time<deadline and improvable:
       improved_any=False
       for d in range(D):
          # line search around current theta[d]
          base=theta[d]
          for sign in (1,-1):
             for mult in (1,2,4):
                new_theta=theta.copy(); new_theta[d]=base + sign*step*mult
                # build f: normalize(base_seq * exp(sum theta_m dir_m))? To avoid rebuilding from f0? Use current theta.
                nf=build(theta)
                sc=score_fast(nf)
                if sc<best_s: accept... improved_any=True; break
          # if no accept, maybe shrink step for dir
       if not improved_any: step*=0.7; if step<1e-4 break
    return best
```
Building f each time O(D n) if sum theta*dirs. D=10, n=8000 ->80k per eval, okay. But many evals. Use precomputed dirs matrix n x D; `np.exp(base_log + theta @ dirs)`. Need base_log = log(f0) with floor. This parametrization may make values negative? exp positive. It preserves positivity. It changes relative masses multiplicatively. Good.

Since f0 length maybe 6121, D=10, building vector 6k*10 per eval; fine. Score FFT 16k. Could do hundreds evals in minutes.

But if optimum requires additive changes not multiplicative, less. Use normalized blend too? Multiplicative good for simplex.

Coordinate line search can be expensive. Simpler: Use random coordinate gradient? We can compute score gradient for param θ? Too much. Just random perturbs a few coordinates and accept if improve (hill climbing). For D=12, each iteration choose random dimension, try ± step, accept. This is easy.
```
theta=np.zeros(D)
```

```
def _sanitize(f):
    f=np.asarray(f,dtype=float).copy()
    if f.size==0: return None
    f=~.nan_to_num? f=np.where(np.isfinite(f), f, 0.0)
    f=np.maximum(f,0.0)
    s=f.sum()
    if s<1e-8: return None
    return f/s
```

Mirror refine implementation details:
```
def _mirror_refine(f0, deadline, seed=1, top_k=12, initial_eta=0.08, no_improve_limit=90.0):
    f0=_sanitize(f0)
    if f0 is None: return None, inf
    n=len(f0)
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    floor=1e-11/max(1,n)
    f=f0.copy(); f=np.maximum(f,floor); f/=f.sum()
    best=f.copy(); best_s=_score_fast(f)
    last_improve=time.monotonic()
    eta=initial_eta
    stall=0
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
        if time.monotonic()-last_improve > no_improve_limit: break
        b=_conv_square(f)
        if b.size==0: break
        M=float(b.max())
        if not finite: break
        # active top
        if top_k >= len(b): idx=np.arange(len(b)) else idx=np.argpartition(b, -top_k)[-top_k:]
        vals=b[idx]
        temp=M*0.015
        if temp < b.max()*1e-12 or not finite: temp=1e-300
        p=np.exp((vals-vals.max())/temp)
        ps=p.sum()
        if ps<=0: p=np.ones(lenidx)/lenidx else p/=ps
        w=np.zeros(len(b)); w[idx]=p
        h=_corr_valid(w,f)
        grad=2.0*h - 2.0*np.mean(h)
        gmax=np.max(np.abs(grad))
        if gmax<1e-18:
            f=(1-0.01)*f+0.01*rng.random(n); f=sanitize; stall++ continue
        grad=grad/gmax
        improved=False
        for e in (eta, 0.5*eta):
            lf=np.log(f) - e*grad
            lf-=lf.max()
            nf=np.exp(lf)
            nf=np.maximum(nf, floor); s=nf.sum(); if s<=0: continue
            nf/=s
            sc=_score_fast(nf)
            if sc < best_s - 1e-12:
                f=nf; best=nf.copy(); best_s=sc; last_improve=time.monotonic(); improved=True; break
        if improved:
            stall=0
            if eta<0.18: eta*=1.03
        else:
            stall+=1
            eta = max(0.004, eta*0.9)
            if stall > 8:
                # perturb from best
                d=_smooth_direction(n,rng,8)
                alpha=min(0.05,0.004*stall)
                lf=np.log(best) - alpha*d
                lf-=lf.max(); nf=exp...
                if sc<best_s+0.01? just set f=nf (even if worse) to explore
                stall=0
    # final one evaluation
    if _score_fast(f)<best_s: ...
    return best,best_s
```
Using best to perturb if not improve; keeping f maybe worse. Good.

Smooth direction:
```
def _smooth_direction(n, rng, modes=8):
    x=np.arange(n,dtype=float)/float(max(1,n))
    d=np.zeros(n)
    for m in range(1,modes+1):
        amp=rng.normal(0.0,1.0)/float(m+1)
        phase=rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0*math.pi)
        d += amp*np.cos(2.0*math.pi*m*x + phase)
    d += rng.normal(0.0,0.5)*(np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)-0.5)
    d -= d.mean()
    scale=np.max(np.abs(d))
    if scale>0: d/=scale
    return d
```

Subspace refine:
```
def _subspace_refine(f0, deadline, seed=2, D=10, no_improve_limit=120.0):
    f0=_sanitize; n; rng; floor; base=f0+floor; base/=base.sum(); logbase=np.log(base)
    dirs=[_smooth_direction(n,rng,6+ (m%5)) for m in range(D)]
    coeffs=np.vstack(dirs) # shape D,n
    theta=np.zeros(D)
    best_f=f0.copy(); best_s=_score_fast(f0); last=time
    step=0.05
    noimprove=0
    while time<deadline:
       if time-last>no_improve_limit: break
       d=rng.integers(D)
       # generate 3 tries
       improved=False
       for _ in range(3):
          cand=theta.copy()
          if rng.random()<0.2:
             # random full
             cand += np.clip(rng.normal(0, step/np.sqrt(D), D), -0.8, 0.8)
          else:
             cand[d] += rng.normal(0, step)
          cand=np.clip(cand, -2.5,2.5)
          v=logbase + cand @ coeffs
          v-=v.max(); nf=exp(v); nf=max(floor); sc=score
          if sc<best_s-1e-12:
             theta=cand; best_f=nf; best_s=sc; last=time; improved=True; break
       if not improved:
          noimprove+=1
          if noimprove>10:
             # larger jump
             step=min(0.5, step*1.5); noimprove=0
          else:
             step=max(0.01, step*0.95)
    return best_f,best_s
```
Candidate with worse not accepted; hill-climbing. If stuck, large jump. Good.

```
def _C_poly_sequence(n, coeff):
  c=_C_sequence(n)
  f=np.zeros(n)
  for m,q in enumerate(coeff):
     if q==0: continue
     if m==0: f += q*c
     else: f[m:] += q*c[:n-m]
  if np.min(f)<-1e-7: return None
  f=np.maximum(f,0)
  return _normalize(f)
```

```
while time<deadline:
  for d in 1..3 (or random):
     for sign in ±:
       cand=best_c.copy(); cand[d]=best_c[d]+sign*step
       f=C_poly(n,cand); if f none continue; sc=score
       if sc<best_s: accept; improved=True; break
  if not improved: step*=0.9; if step<1e-5 break; occasional larger
return best_f,best_s
```

```python
"""
Search for a non-negative sequence minimizing
    2*n*max(conv(a,a))/(sum(a)**2).
Strategy:
  * Use several structured starting points, especially the binomial/inverse-sqrt
    family C_i = binom(2i,i)/4^i, which is close to the relevant limit.
  * Run a short structured hill-climb on low-degree polynomial multipliers of C_i.
    This gives a few better “near-flat” convolution bases.
  * Use a full-dimensional mirror-descent local optimizer with active top-lag
    gradients (evaluated by FFT) and adaptive random perturbations.
  * Use a low-dimensional multiplicative subspace optimizer for additional polish.
The best sequence found up to the time limit is returned.
"""

import numpy as np
import math
import time


def _next_pow2(m):
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def _conv_square(f):
    n = len(f)
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=float)
    size = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    F = np.fft.rfft(f, size)
    b = np.fft.irfft(F * F, size)
    return b[:2 * n - 1]


def _score_fast(f):
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=float)
    n = f.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")
    s = float(np.sum(f))
    if s <= 1e-12 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return float("inf")
    b = _conv_square(f)
    if b.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    m = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * m / (s * s))


def _corr_valid(r, f):
    """
    Return h[i] = sum_{j=0}^{n-1} r[i+j] f[j], for r length >= n.
    This is the valid part needed for gradient 2*correlate(r, f).
    """
    n = len(f)
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=float)
    size = _next_pow2(3 * n - 2)
    R = np.fft.rfft(r, size)
    F = np.fft.rfft(f, size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(R * np.conj(F), size)
    return c[:n]


def _sanitize(f):
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=float).copy()
    if f.size == 0:
        return None
    f = np.where(np.isfinite(f), f, 0.0)
    f = np.maximum(f, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(f))
    if s <= 1e-10:
        return None
    return f / s


def _add_candidate(cands, f, name):
    fs = _sanitize(f)
    if fs is None:
        return
    sc = _score_fast(fs)
    if np.isfinite(sc):
        cands.append((sc, len(cands), name, fs))


def _C_sequence(n):
    c = np.empty(n, dtype=float)
    if n > 0:
        c[0] = 1.0
    for i in range(1, n):
        c[i] = c[i - 1] * float(2 * i - 1) / float(2 * i)
    return c


def _C_poly_from_c(c, coeff):
    """
    f_i = sum_m coeff[m] * C_{i-m}, with C_t=0 for t<0.
    Reject if the unclipped vector has meaningful negative entries.
    """
    n = len(c)
    f = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    for m, q in enumerate(coeff):
        if q == 0.0:
            continue
        if m >= n:
            continue
        if m == 0:
            f += q * c
        else:
            f[m:] += q * c[:n - m]
    if np.any(f < -1e-7):
        return None
    f = np.maximum(f, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(f))
    if s <= 1e-10:
        return None
    return f / s


def _trig_candidate(n, amp, beta=0.0):
    if n < 2:
        return None
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=float) / float(n - 1)
    f = 1.0 + amp * np.cos(math.pi * x)
    if beta != 0.0:
        f = f + beta * np.cos(2.0 * math.pi * x)
    return _sanitize(f)


def _power_candidate(n, p=0.5):
    i = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=float)
    return i ** (-p)


def _smooth_direction(n, rng, modes=8):
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=float) / float(max(1, n))
    d = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    for m in range(1, modes + 1):
        amp = rng.normal(0.0, 1.0) / float(m + 1)
        phase = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * math.pi)
        d += amp * np.cos(2.0 * math.pi * m * x + phase)
    d += rng.normal(0.0, 0.5) * (np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n) - 0.5)
    d -= d.mean()
    scale = float(np.max(np.abs(d)))
    if scale > 1e-30:
        d /= scale
    return d


def _C_poly_refine(n, c, deadline, seed=11, degree=5):
    """
    Structured hill-climb on low-degree polynomial multipliers of C.
    This is motivated by asymptotic tail control of the flat-square sequence.
    """
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    coeff = np.zeros(degree + 1, dtype=float)
    coeff[0] = 1.0

    best_f = _C_poly_from_c(c, coeff)
    if best_f is None:
        return None, float("inf")
    best_s = _score_fast(best_f)
    best_coeff = coeff.copy()

    exp = time.monotonic()
    last_improve = exp
    step = 0.035
    no_improve = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if time.monotonic() - last_improve > 75.0:
            break

        cand_coeff = best_coeff.copy()
        improved = False

        # Try a few random perturbations around the current best coefficients.
        for _ in range(6):
            d = int(rng.integers(1, len(cand_coeff)))
            cand_coeff[d] = best_coeff[d] + rng.normal(0.0, step)
            f = _C_poly_from_c(c, cand_coeff)
            if f is None:
                continue
            sc = _score_fast(f)
            if sc < best_s - 1e-12:
                best_s = sc
                best_f = f.copy()
                best_coeff = cand_coeff.copy()
                last_improve = time.monotonic()
                improved = True
                break

        if improved:
            no_improve = 0
            if step < 0.12:
                step *= 1.05
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            # Occasional larger exploratory jump.
            if no_improve == 6:
                cand_coeff = best_coeff.copy()
                cand_coeff[1:] += rng.normal(0.0, 0.12, len(cand_coeff) - 1)
                f = _C_poly_from_c(c, cand_coeff)
                if f is not None:
                    sc = _score_fast(f)
                    if sc < best_s - 1e-12:
                        best_s = sc
                        best_f = f.copy()
                        best_coeff = cand_coeff.copy()
                        last_improve = time.monotonic()
                        improved = True

            if not improved:
                step = max(0.002, step * 0.88)

    return best_f, best_s


def _mirror_refine(f0, deadline, seed=21, top_k=12, initial_eta=0.08,
                   no_improve_limit=90.0):
    """
    Mirror-descent (exponentiated gradient) local search.
    Active top convolution lags define the subgradient direction.
    """
    fs = _sanitize(f0)
    if fs is None:
        return None, float("inf")

    n = len(fs)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    floor = 1e-12 / max(1, n)
    f = fs.copy()
    f = np.maximum(f, floor)
    f /= f.sum()

    best_f = f.copy()
    best_s = _score_fast(f)
    last_improve = time.monotonic()
    eta = initial_eta
    stall = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if time.monotonic() - last_improve > no_improve_limit:
            break

        b = _conv_square(f)
        if b.size == 0:
            break
        M = float(np.max(b))
        if not np.isfinite(M):
            break

        if top_k >= len(b):
            idx = np.arange(len(b), dtype=int)
        else:
            idx = np.argpartition(b, -top_k)[-top_k:]

        vals = b[idx]
        temp = M * 0.015
        if temp <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(temp):
            temp = 1e-30

        p = np.exp((vals - vals.max()) / temp)
        ps = float(p.sum())
        if ps <= 1e-30:
            p = np.ones(len(idx), dtype=float) / float(len(idx))
        else:
            p /= ps

        w = np.zeros(len(b), dtype=float)
        w[idx] = p

        h = _corr_valid(w, f)
        grad = 2.0 * h - 2.0 * float(np.mean(h))
        gmax = float(np.max(np.abs(grad)))
        if gmax < 1e-18:
            # Very flat gradient: perturb a little.
            d = _smooth_direction(n, rng, 6)
            alpha = 0.02
            lf = np.log(f) - alpha * d
            lf -= lf.max()
            nf = np.exp(lf)
            nf = np.maximum(nf, floor)
            nf /= nf.sum()
            f = nf
            stall += 1
            continue

        grad = grad / gmax

        improved = False
        for e in (eta, 0.5 * eta):
            lf = np.log(f) - e * grad
            lf -= lf.max()
            nf = np.exp(lf)
            nf = np.maximum(nf, floor)
            s = float(nf.sum())
            if s <= 1e-30:
                continue
            nf /= s
            sc = _score_fast(nf)
            if sc < best_s - 1e-12:
                f = nf
                best_f = nf.copy()
                best_s = sc
                last_improve = time.monotonic()
                improved = True
                break

        if improved:
            stall = 0
            if eta < 0.18:
                eta *= 1.03
        else:
            stall += 1
            eta = max(0.004, eta * 0.90)
            if stall > 8:
                d = _smooth_direction(n, rng, 8)
                alpha = min(0.06, 0.005 * stall)
                lf = np.log(best_f) - alpha * d
                lf -= lf.max()
                nf = np.exp(lf)
                nf = np.maximum(nf, floor)
                nf /= nf.sum()
                f = nf
                stall = 0

    sc = _score_fast(f)
    if sc < best_s:
        best_s = sc
        best_f = f.copy()

    best_f = np.maximum(best_f, 0.0)
    bs = float(np.sum(best_f))
    if bs > 1e-12:
        best_f /= bs

    return best_f, best_s


def _subspace_refine(f0, deadline, seed=31, D=10, no_improve_limit=120.0):
    """
    Low-dimensional multiplicative perturbation:
        f(theta) = normalize(exp(log(f0) + theta @ directions))
    Random coordinate hill-climbing in theta.
    """
    fs = _sanitize(f0)
    if fs is None:
        return None, float("inf")

    n = len(fs)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    floor = 1e-12 / max(1, n)

    base = fs.copy()
    base = np.maximum(base, floor)
    base /= base.sum()
    logbase = np.log(base)

    dirs = []
    for m in range(D):
        dirs.append(_smooth_direction(n, rng, 5 + (m % 6)))
    coeffs = np.vstack(dirs)

    theta = np.zeros(D, dtype=float)

    best_f = base.copy()
    best_s = _score_fast(best_f)
    last_improve = time.monotonic()
    step = 0.055
    no_improve = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if time.monotonic() - last_improve > no_improve_limit:
            break

        improved = False

        for _ in range(4):
            cand_theta = theta.copy()
            if rng.random() < 0.20:
                cand_theta += np.clip(rng.normal(0.0, step / math.sqrt(max(1, D)), D),
                                      -0.8, 0.8)
            else:
                d = int(rng.integers(D))
                cand_theta[d] = theta[d] + rng.normal(0.0, step)

            cand_theta = np.clip(cand_theta, -2.5, 2.5)

            v = logbase + cand_theta @ coeffs
            v -= v.max()
            nf = np.exp(v)
            nf = np.maximum(nf, floor)
            s = float(nf.sum())
            if s <= 1e-30:
                continue
            nf /= s
            sc = _score_fast(nf)

            if sc < best_s - 1e-12:
                best_f = nf.copy()
                best_s = sc
                theta = cand_theta.copy()
                last_improve = time.monotonic()
                improved = True
                break

        if improved:
            no_improve = 0
            if step < 0.16:
                step *= 1.04
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve == 8:
                # Larger exploratory jump.
                cand_theta = theta + np.clip(rng.normal(0.0, 0.25, D), -0.9, 0.9)
                cand_theta = np.clip(cand_theta, -2.5, 2.5)
                v = logbase + cand_theta @ coeffs
                v -= v.max()
                nf = np.exp(v)
                nf = np.maximum(nf, floor)
                s = float(nf.sum())
                if s > 1e-30:
                    nf /= s
                    sc = _score_fast(nf)
                    if sc < best_s - 1e-12:
                        best_f = nf.copy()
                        best_s = sc
                        theta = cand_theta.copy()
                        last_improve = time.monotonic()
                        improved = True

            if not improved:
                step = max(0.008, step * 0.88)

    best_f = np.maximum(best_f, 0.0)
    bs = float(np.sum(best_f))
    if bs > 1e-12:
        best_f /= bs

    return best_f, best_s


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Search for the best non-negative sequence and return it as a list of floats.
    """
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    hard_deadline = start_time + float(budget_s) - 10.0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    candidates = []

    # ---- Add explicitly provided previous construction, if available. --------
    given = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    given_len = 0
    if isinstance(given, (list, tuple, np.ndarray)):
        gf = _sanitize(given)
        if gf is not None:
            given_len = len(gf)
            _add_candidate(candidates, gf, "given_length_%d" % given_len)

    # ---- Choose a few useful lengths. ---------------------------------------
    lengths = [4096, 8192]
    if given_len > 2000 and given_len < 12000:
        lengths.append(given_len)

    # De-duplicate while preserving order.
    uniq_lengths = []
    for L in lengths:
        if L not in uniq_lengths:
            uniq_lengths.append(L)

    # ---- Basic structured seeds. -------------------------------------------
    for L in uniq_lengths:
        c = _C_sequence(L)
        _add_candidate(candidates, c, "C_%d" % L)
        _add_candidate(candidates, _trig_candidate(L, 1.0, 0.0), "trig1_%d" % L)
        _add_candidate(candidates, _trig_candidate(L, 0.85, 0.0), "trig85_%d" % L)
        _add_candidate(candidates, _trig_candidate(L, 0.7, 0.1), "trig70b_%d" % L)
        _add_candidate(candidates, _power_candidate(L, 0.5), "power50_%d" % L)
        d1 = _smooth_direction(L, rng, 10)
        _add_candidate(candidates, np.exp(0.7 * d1), "rand1_%d" % L)
        d2 = _smooth_direction(L, rng, 14)
        _add_candidate(candidates, np.exp(0.9 * d2), "rand2_%d" % L)

    candidates.sort()
    best_score = float("inf")
    best_f = None

    # Initial best from explicit seeds.
    if candidates:
        best_score = candidates[0][0]
        best_f = candidates[0][3].copy()

    # ---- Structured C-polynomial refinement on selected lengths. ------------
    # This often produces a markedly better base than raw C.
    poly_lengths = []
    if given_len > 3000 and given_len < 12000:
        poly_lengths.append(given_len)
    poly_lengths.append(8192)
    if 4096 not in poly_lengths:
        poly_lengths.append(4096)

    # Keep unique.
    upoly = []
    for L in poly_lengths:
        if L not in upoly:
            upoly.append(L)

    # Allocate modest time to the most promising lengths first, but always
    # reserve time for full-dimensional polishing.
    for idx, L in enumerate(upoly):
        if time.monotonic() > hard_deadline - 900.0:
            break
        alloc = 90.0 if idx == 0 else 70.0
        dl = time.monotonic() + alloc
        c = _C_sequence(L)
        pf, ps = _C_poly_refine(L, c, dl, seed=100 + idx, degree=5)
        if pf is not None:
            _add_candidate(candidates, pf, "Cpoly_%d" % L)

    candidates.sort()

    # Refresh global best after structured construction.
    if candidates and candidates[0][0] < best_score:
        best_score = candidates[0][0]
        best_f = candidates[0][3].copy()
    elif best_f is None and candidates:
        best_score = candidates[0][0]
        best_f = candidates[0][3].copy()

    # ---- Choose a few sequences to polish. ---------------------------------
    selected = []
    selected_names = []

    if candidates:
        selected.append(candidates[0])
        selected_names.append(candidates[0][2])

    # Include the best raw C if promising and not already selected.
    for item in candidates:
        if item[0] < best_score + 0.12 and item[2].startswith("C_") and item[2] not in selected_names:
            selected.append(item)
            selected_names.append(item[2])
            break

    # Include best C-poly if promising and not already selected.
    for item in candidates:
        if item[0] < best_score + 0.10 and item[2].startswith("Cpoly") and item[2] not in selected_names:
            selected.append(item)
            selected_names.append(item[2])
            break

    # Add up to two more globally good sequences.
    for item in candidates:
        if len(selected) >= 4:
            break
        if item[0] < best_score + 0.08 and item[2] not in selected_names:
            selected.append(item)
            selected_names.append(item[2])

    # ---- Mirror-refine top candidates. --------------------------------------
    mirror_allocs = [600.0, 430.0, 320.0, 220.0]
    for idx, item in enumerate(selected):
        if time.monotonic() > hard_deadline - 300.0:
            break
        alloc = mirror_allocs[idx] if idx < len(mirror_allocs) else 180.0
        alloc = min(alloc, max(60.0, hard_deadline - time.monotonic() - 60.0))
        if alloc < 45.0:
            break
        dl = time.monotonic() + alloc
        rf, rs = _mirror_refine(item[3], dl, seed=500 + idx,
                                top_k=12,
                                initial_eta=0.08,
                                no_improve_limit=85.0)
        if rf is not None and rs < best_score:
            best_score = rs
            best_f = rf.copy()

    # ---- Extra polishing of the current best. -------------------------------
    if best_f is not None and time.monotonic() < hard_deadline - 220.0:
        alloc = min(420.0, hard_deadline - time.monotonic() - 80.0)
        if alloc > 120.0:
            rf, rs = _mirror_refine(best_f, time.monotonic() + alloc,
                                    seed=757, top_k=10,
                                    initial_eta=0.05,
                                    no_improve_limit=100.0)
            if rf is not None and rs < best_score:
                best_score = rs
                best_f = rf.copy()

    if best_f is not None and time.monotonic() < hard_deadline - 160.0:
        alloc = min(360.0, hard_deadline - time.monotonic() - 40.0)
        if alloc > 90.0:
            sf, ss = _subspace_refine(best_f, time.monotonic() + alloc,
                                      seed=911, D=10,
                                      no_improve_limit=130.0)
            if sf is not None and ss < best_score:
                best_score = ss
                best_f = sf.copy()

    if best_f is not None and time.monotonic() < hard_deadline - 80.0:
        alloc = min(70.0, hard_deadline - time.monotonic() - 10.0)
        if alloc > 30.0:
            rf, rs = _mirror_refine(best_f, time.monotonic() + alloc,
                                    seed=1200, top_k=8,
                                    initial_eta=0.03,
                                    no_improve_limit=25.0)
            if rf is not None and rs < best_score:
                best_score = rs
                best_f = rf.copy()

    # ---- Final fallback and sanitation. -------------------------------------
    if best_f is None:
        c = _C_sequence(4096)
        best_f = c
        best_score = _score_fast(best_f)

    best_f = _sanitize(best_f)
    if best_f is None:
        return [0.01] * 10

    # Final score is scale-invariant; return a normalized list.
    final = [float(max(0.0, min(1000.0, x))) for x in best_f]

    # If the evaluator is available, do a sanity check, but do not fail if it
    # cannot be called in this environment.
    if "evaluate_sequence" in globals():
        try:
            eval_score = evaluate_sequence(final)
        except Exception:
            eval_score = float("nan")
        if not np.isfinite(eval_score):
            # Extremely unlikely; keep the sanitized sequence.
            pass

    return final
```
